# Large routed tile and compact layer frontier

Partial unified optimization execution. V4 adds a 4,096-token routed tile (maximum live tile 4,351 after merging only sub-256 tails), optional release of obsolete layer-frontier arrays before expert assembly, actual Workspace C GPU-wait/CPU row-sort observations, and typed memory/pass observations for context-check. Defaults remain off.

With the 4,096-token tile and compact frontier enabled, the 8,192-token numerical probe passed 117 assertions, the generator cancellation/retry probe passed 593 and the synthetic image-span/MTP recovery probe passed 240. Candidate final logits, logical state and routing stayed exactly equal to the direct 4,096-token reference in the numerical probe. This direct-model check does not raise public scheduling or context bounds.

The three-arm latency/resource pilot did not produce a complete clean pair for either preregistered comparison. Only the first compact-frontier cell had no swap activity; all other cells were excluded. The raw times, read counts and physical samples are retained and do not support a speed or memory-saving claim. The 4,096-token tile and compact frontier are unqualified for adoption. In particular, a lower expert-record count and favorable-looking excluded times are not evidence of a delivered request-speed improvement.

Subsequent timing blocks will avoid concurrent workspace shell commands as an additional contamination control. The existing global swap-counter exclusion remains unchanged. A separate chronological-prefill memory calibration will test whether compact state/bounded indexer/PLE allow a larger ordinary pass inside the existing 10 GB target, before changing any planner constants.
